Meter Work - Meter Relocation 
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒  
Estimated Start Date:  
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Saadat Khan 
Business Contact: Carmen Fong 
 
Work Description:  
The meter relocation program aims to move inside meters sets to outside the premises.  Facilities to be relocated include the 
meter, associated appurtenances and, piping from the point of entry to the pre-existing point of connection to customer-
owned piping.  
 
Justification Summary:  
This program is focused on the safety of gas customers and is a requirement of the Company’s current Joint Proposal adopted 
in Case 19-G-0310. When renewing services where meters are located inside the customer’s premise, all reasonable efforts 
will be made to relocate meters to the outside with priority given to high pressure services. Consideration for relocation 
opportunities include: 
• 
Renewal of bare steel services; 
• 
Service renewals involving leak prone pipe; 
• 
Service renewals in “Flood Zones”; 
• 
Removal/replacement of mercury regulators upon discovery; 
• 
Repairs on leaking services; 
• 
Interruptions of service to customers who repeatedly deny access for mandated safety inspections; or 
• 
When inspection determines the service is located in a corrosive environment such as: in the vicinity of certain 
chemical processing operations (e.g., electroplating, dry cleaning chemicals, etc.). 
 
The following exceptions apply to all meter relocation efforts: 
• 
where the customer refuses to provide consent to such relocation;  
• 
where local building codes, regulations, or authorities preclude such relocations;  
• 
where exterior or interior obstacles, space constraints, or physical barriers preclude such relocations;  
• 
when the work involved is an emergency service line repair or replacement;  
• 
where relocation requires extensive interior or exterior restoration and/or complicated interior piping work that 
would involve excessive costs or present increased operational risks for the Company and/or customer; or  
• 
where the gas meter should not be moved outside for safety reasons. 
 
Indoor gas meters must be inspected for safety at a minimum every five years.  An appointment is scheduled to conduct 
meter inspections, and the customer must be on the premises for up to a four-hour period while the inspection is completed. 
Therefore, it is recommended to move the meters out, where possible, to further enhance safety, especially in the event a 
future leak may develop, and for to conduct these periodic inspections without inconveniencing the customer.  Additional 
benefits to outside meter sets include allowing regular access to the meter for reading and verification and no cost to the 
customer for mandatory annual and/or 5-year leak surveys/corrosion inspections. 
Exhibit____(GIOP-5) 
Page 134 of 273


--- Page 264 ---
Customer Benefits: 
This program benefits the customer by making meter sets accessible to Field Operations without entering the customer’s 
building.  The gas customer does not need to be on the premises for Field Operations to carry out mandated safety 
inspections.  In the case of a more severe gas incident where access to the meter set is required, the customer does not need 
to be on the premises to allow Field Operations access.  As a result, the customer has more trust in our ability to mitigate any 
potential incidents regardless of their location on or off the premises.

NYS PSC OQ Ruling:   
Learning & Development - Tools & Equipment  
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☐ Capital  ☒ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230020361 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect ☒ OQP 
Annual Program: ☒

Estimated In-Service Date:

Work Description:  
This program requests incremental funding for tools, equipment and materials purchases, upgrades to support training, 
testing field personnel in compliance with existing, and new OQ programs under the NYS PSC OQ Ruling in Case 19-G-0736.  
Funding will accommodate existing personnel and new expected clients (e.g. contractors, engineers, etc.). 
  
Justification Summary:  
The resources described within this document are for: 1) the continuing support of current Gas Technical Training initiatives 
and 2) the new regulatory and compliance training requirements of the OQ Ruling.  Due to increasing demands of the industry 
and newly adopted regulations, Technical Training needs to increase and enhance its training resources. This proposal will 
allow for NY Technical Training in DNY to perform all required regulatory and compliance instructor led training, laboratory 
training, OQ hands on performance evaluations, and field structured on the job Training for National Grid employees and 
contractors.  
 
Tools & Equipment funding is needed to maintain existing equipment, provide new or replace worn or defective equipment, 
and continue to fund all DNY Technical Training resources needed to deliver mandatory regulatory and compliance training.  
This will allow Technical Training to continually train and qualify existing and new employees and contractors throughout the 
year.    
 
Customer Benefits: 
Proper funding will ensure that employees and contractors working on National Grid gas infrastructure are appropriately 
trained in accordance with applicable state and federal regulations.   The hands on and field evaluation process provides 
another layer of inspection and assurance of proper installation which ensures compliance with all policies, procedures, 
standards, and newly issued mandates.  Proper funding for training will also increase the integrity and safety of installed gas 
infrastructure.

Work Description:

new OQ programs under the PSC Ruling in Case 19-G-0736. FTEs will accommodate existing personnel, new expected clients 
(e.g. contractors, engineers, etc.), and the additional supporting workload required by the order.  
 
Justification Summary:

development of program materials, training program scheduling, additional instructor led training, additional hands-on 
performance evaluations, and compliance with new evaluation ratios.  The requested FTEs will accommodate existing internal 
employees as well as contractors, to the extent as determined necessary. 
 
Due to increasing demands of the industry and newly adopted regulations, Technical Training needs to increase and enhance 
its training resources.   This proposal will allow for NY Technical Training in DNY to perform all required regulatory and 
compliance instructor led training, laboratory training, OQ hands on performance evaluations, and field structured on the job 
Training for National Grid employees and contractors.

another layer of Inspection and assurance of proper installation which ensures compliance with all policies, procedures, 
standards, and newly issued mandates.  Proper funding for training will also increase the integrity and safety of installed gas 
infrastructure.

Pipeline Integrity - IMP - Drip Pot 
Modifications 
  
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230000038 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐ 
Estimated Start Date: April, 2024 
Estimated In-Service Date: As 
Completed  
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
This program replaces pipeline drips, valves, and associated equipment, as required. 
 
Justification Summary:  
Both Federal (CFR 192) and State (NYCRR 255) regulations require operators to perform inspection and maintenance of their 
pipeline components.  Anomalies discovered during inspections are evaluated, prioritized, and mitigated in accordance with 
these rules and the Company’s procedures. 
 
This mandated work is to replace non-functioning or leaking transmission drips (liquid removal assemblies) . This can be 
accomplished by replacement of components or complete removal and replacement from the pipeline. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
ILI enablement will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission system.  Through ILI 
enablement, the Company is able to proactively inspect existing transmission assets to identify anomalies or other issues that 
might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our customers.

Pipeline Integrity - IMP - GM 2 - ILI enablement 
from NWN to Stewart Ave 
  
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230101749 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐  
Estimated Start Date: April 2023 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
March 2028 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
Pipeline Integrity - IMP - GM 2 -ILI enablement from NWN to Stewart Ave Gas Regulation Station.  This project will remove 
and replace any valve, fitting, or section of pipeline which will prevent the passage of a free-swimming ILI. 
 
Justification Summary:  
Mandated project to make existing pipeline ILI enabled.  As a result of the San Bruno incident the NTSB and PHMSA have 
issued guidance that all operators make as much of their gas transmission systems ILI enabled as possible.  This was 
reinforced with the language contained in the PHMSA MEGA Rule in RIN1. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
ILI enablement will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission system.  Through ILI 
enablement, the Company is able to proactively inspect existing transmission assets to identify anomalies or other issues 
that might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our customers.

Pipeline Integrity - IVP - GM 9 Stewart Ave 
to Merrick  
  
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230001781 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☒ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐  
Estimated Start Date: April 2023 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
March 2028 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
This project will install approximately 8270 ft of 20” steel gas main with “Free Swim ILI” capability to replace the existing 16” 
main.  This section was identified by National Grid’s IVP Plan and records review / records verification as a section installed 
from Stewart Ave Gate Station to Merrick Ave (vicinity of Eisenhower Park) partially using A139 pipe and mechanical couplings 
for joining, and without adequate pressure test records .  This project will also eliminate the construction and manufacturing 
threat. 
 
Justification Summary: 
The recent PHMSA rule making (9 RIN1) has directed operators to address pipelines that do not have adequate or missing 
records such as pressure test charts, material reports and weld records.  Compliance requirements are for 50% of inventory 
to be addressed by 2027 and 100% to be addressed by 2035.  A comprehensive records review undertaken by the company 
has identified that this pipeline has inadequate records. 
 
Customer Benefits 
Replacement of pipe with inadequate records will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission 
system.  Through replacement, the Company is able to comply with new federal code requirements and address material 
issues that might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our 
customers.

Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐  
Estimated Start Date: October 2022 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
March 2030 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
As a result of External Direct Assessment (ECDA ) inspections completed in calendar year 2021, it was determined that the 
entire GM 10 should be replaced because the pipe was fabricated using spiral wound weld method.  Industry experience with 
spiral welded pipe has shown that is prone to failures at the weld seam due to porosity and linear flaws.  This project will also 
incorporate the 3 GM 10 Part R projects (GM-10 Part-R Midian St, GM-10 Part-R William St, GM-10 Part-R Camp Ave) these 
locations do not have adequate records to meet current traceable, verifiable, and complete requirements. 
 
Justification Summary:   
Due to results from the most recent ECDA performed as part of Company’s IMP program, inspection results on the long seam 
indicated areas of possible concern.  A follow up fit for service study has indicated that pipeline has linear flaws in the long 
seam.  The study also concluded that the pipeline is safe to operate in the short term, but it is close end of its useful life and 
should be scheduled for replacement. 
 
Customer Benefits 
Replacement of pipe with inadequate records will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission 
system.  Through replacement, the Company is able to comply with new federal code requirements and address material 
issues that might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our 
customers.

Pipeline Integrity - IVP - GM 35 Dix Hills to 
Elwood 
  
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230001782 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐   
Estimated Start Date:  April 2024 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
April 2029 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
This program is to replace pipe that was determined to have insufficient records.  The pipe will be replaced with pipe meeting 
current regulatory requirements. 
 
Justification Summary: 
The recent PHMSA rule making (MEGA Rule-RIN 1 ) has directed operators to address pipelines that do not have adequate or 
missing records such as pressure test charts, material reports, and weld records.  Compliance requirements are for 50% of 
inventory to be addressed by 2027 and 100% to be addressed by 2035.  A comprehensive records review undertaken by the 
Company has identified that this pipeline has inadequate records. 
 
Customer Benefits:  
Replacement of pipe with inadequate records will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission 
system.  Through replacement, the Company is able to comply with new federal code requirements and address material 
issues that might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our 
customers.

Work Description: 
This project will replace 10 feet of 16-inch pipe on Long Island Avenue interconnecting GM-22 to GM-26. 
 
Justification Summary:  
The recent PHMSA rulemaking has directed operators to address pipelines that do not have adequate or missing records such 
as pressure test charts, material reports and weld records.  Compliance requirements are for 50% of inventory to be addressed 
by 2027 and 100% to be addressed by 2035.  A comprehensive records review undertaken by the Company has identified that 
this pipeline has inadequate records and must be replaced.  All projects on the transmission system must be coordinated to 
maintain supply to customers.  A yearly outage plan is established and each project is given a window during which the project 
must be executed. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
Replacement of pipe with inadequate records will allow for the enhanced operational safety of the Company’s transmission 
system.  Through replacement, the Company is able to comply with new Federal code requirements and address material 
issues that might adversely affect the integrity of the system and result in gas safety concerns for the system and our 
customers.

Pipeline Integrity - IVP - Reactive Main Replacement  
  
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230002019 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒  
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description: 
This program provides funding to complete necessary work following pipe condition assessments performed under the IMP 
program.  Inspections using ILI and ECDA technology are used to identify sections of pipe with possible anomalies.  Follow up 
excavations determine the type of remediation required.  Sections of pipe that cannot be repaired with insitu methods must 
be replaced by cylinder cut outs.  The reactive main replacement item funds this type of mitigative activity. 
 
Justification   
Both Federal (CFR 192) and State (NYCRR 255) regulations require operators to perform inspection and maintenance of their 
pipeline systems.  Anomalies discovered during inspections are evaluated, prioritized, and mitigated in accordance with these 
rules and the Company’s procedures. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
Reactive main replacement addresses anomalies found through ECDA and ILI inspection processes to ensure continued safe 
operation of the pipeline sections of the pipeline are replaced.  This results in reduced operational risk and increased safety. 
 
Funding Detail 
 
Cost Breakdown:

Pipeline Integrity - Northwest Nassau - Phase 2B  
GM Down Rate 
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230001971 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐  
Estimated Start Date: In Progress  
Estimated In-Service Date:  
March 2025 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description:  
The Northwest Nassau (NWN) project Phase 2B includes the installation of new transmission piping and replaces the need to 
operate some of the oldest sections of the pipelines on the KEDLI system at transmission pressures by allowing the 
downgrading of some existing infrastructure.  The objective of Phase 2B is to install new transmission piping and downrate 
GM-8, which will allow that main to be tied into the existing 60 psig system and reduce the need to install additional costly 
system reinforcements to address pressure issues in the Westbury area.  NWN Phase 2B is divided into two project execution 
segments: northern miles 1-2 and southern miles 3-4.  Project segments are being executed in parallel requiring multiple 
design and contractor procurement events.  In total, the project will install approximately 4.3 miles of 24-inch, 350 psig gas 
transmission main with connections to GM 1 & 2 and down rate existing gas main GM8.  
 
Justification Summary:  
The subject pipelines were built over various decades beginning in the early 1950s and continuing to the current year.  This 
project addresses the lack of adequate documentation required to validate the maximum allowable operating pressure 
(MAOP), which were not required at the time of installation; lack of adequate documentation of materials used to construct 
these pipelines; and the lack of non-destructive examination (NDE) records for the welded joints for Gas Main 1, 8 and 16 
(GM-1) in northwest Nassau County.  Replacing this pipeline with pipe manufactured under modern processes and controls, 
constructed under modern construction standards, a NDE of welded joints, and a pressure test proving the integrity of the 
pipeline will mitigate the risk of operating the pipeline system in this area. When Phase 2 is completed, pressures will be high 
enough to maintain gas service to all Northwest Nassau customers under all operating conditions.  Phase 2 will down rate 
existing GM-8 and will allow it to be tied into the existing 60 psig system where we have pressure problems in the Westbury 
area thus reducing the need to install additional costly system reinforcements. 
 
 
Customer Benefits:  
This project replaces and/or downgrades the oldest sections of the pipelines on the KEDLI gas transmission system to reduce 
the risk associated with operating the gas transmission system in that area.   This will improve public safety and the reliability 
of the gas system.  By downrating existing pipelines where possible to reinforce the distribution system rather than replacing 
these pipelines, this project also results in the avoidance of future replacement work in the Company’s capital portfolio.

Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
  
Alternative 1:  In-situ testing of materials and pipeline testing.  Establish material properties and re-hydro test each pipeline 
segment.  Alternative #1 leaves in place legacy pipe that is in excess of 50 years old operating at a stress level of 20% and 
greater.  Risk reduction for operating pipelines will not be achieved and capacity and system pressure support will not be 
improved. 
 
Alternative 2: Do Nothing.  This alternative is rejected because new regulations require establishment of complete records 
and MAOP reconfirmation. Additionally, the Company’s review indicates that existing facilities in this area may not be 
sufficient to meet forecasted peak demand in future years, and may require an additional reinforcement project is this 
project is not in-service.

Pipeline Integrity - Northwest Nassau - Transmission Main & Control 
Valve - Phase 3  
 
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230001601 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐  
Estimated Start Date:  April 2022 
Estimated In-Service Date: March 2028 
Sponsor: Kern, Mike 
Business Contact: Conklin, Kevin 
 
Work Description:  
This project addresses three aging natural gas transmission lines in northwestern Nassau County. The project would 
continue the replacement of 8.8 miles of transmission main, install 5.6 miles of new pipe, down rate 9.1 miles of pipe, and 
construct two new regulator stations in response to gaps in records and concerns regarding the materials originally used to 
construct existing mains 1, 8 and 16. The project is planned in three phases: 
 
Phase One (FY 2017-19):  Complete  
 
Phase Two (FY 2019-24): Complete  
• Install approximately 3.9 miles of 24” 350 psig main. Route on Clinton Avenue/Glen Cove Road from Stewart Ave to the 
Long Island Expressway (LIE). 
• Downrate the existing 16” main from 350 psig and operate at 60 psig, tie into the local distribution system and certify as a 
124/60 main.  
 
Phase Three (FY 2021-24):  
• Relay approximately 4.8 miles of Gas Main 1 along the LIE From Lake Success to Roslyn Heights with 24” 350 psig main. 
Downrate and operate the existing 16” main at 60 psig, and certify as a 124/60 main.  
• Some additional 60 psig distribution system reinforcements will not be required as a result of the down rating to 124/60.  
 
Justification Summary:  
In response to the events of San Bruno and PHMSA’s integrity verification program (IVP) recommendations, the Company has 
undertaken a review of historical records to substantiate the maximum allowable operating pressure (MAOP) of its gas 
transmission mains, as well as a review of each pipe’s operating history.  The results of this review indicated the replacement 
and down rating of Gas Mains 1, 8, and 16 will mitigate system risks, increase system reliability, and comply with the recently 
enacted PHMSA code requirements in 49 CFR 192.607 & 192.624. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
The program seeks to further reduce the risk of operating the gas transmission system, which will improve public safety and 
the reliability of the gas delivery system.  By downrating existing pipelines where possible, to reinforce the distribution 
system rather than replacing these transmission pipelines, this project also results in avoidance of future replacement work.

Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
  
Alternative 1:   Re-test each pipeline segment.  As an alternative, KEDLI could establish properties and re-hydro test each 
pipeline segment.  This alternative would leave in place pipe that is over 50 years old operating at a stress level of 20 percent 
and greater.  This option is rejected because it does not achieve the Company’s risk reduction goals. 
 
Alternative 2: Do Nothing.  This alternative is rejected because new regulations require establishment of complete records 
and MAOP reconfirmation.  Additionally, the Company’s review indicates that existing facilities in this area may not be 
sufficient to meet forecasted peak demand in future years and may require an additional reinforcement project is this project 
is not in-service.

Proactive Regulator Stations 
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230000059 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☒ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date: 
 
Sponsor: Leyble, Dennis  
 
Business Contact: Adesina, Risikat  
 
Work Description:  
This is an annual capital improvement program.  To determine the work plan, all regulating stations on Long Island are 
inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years. Using data from the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic 
Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections, technical assessments will be made for each pressure 
regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, regulator performance, and corrosion data. 
This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a station outage will be used to prioritize and 
schedule projects within the capital Improvement plan.  Individual work plans for each station will be developed based on 
the station assessment and risk score; however, possible work performed under this program could include replacements to 
aging station equipment such as regulators, valves, and vent poles. 
 
Justification Summary:  
The work performed under this program will include necessary upgrades to the regulating stations across the Long Island 
system based on the results of the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and 
on-site inspections.  This work is necessary to ensure the continued safe and reliable operation of the gas system. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
Having a robust capital improvement program will improve asset condition and the safety of our assets, and address any open 
reliability concerns.

The total spend in FY25 is lower because certain projects are completed outside of the program.

Alternatives

safe and reliable gas service, nor the longer-term objective of improving the operation and performance of the pressure 
regulating stations.  The consequences of not completing the work scheduled will result in increased risks associated with the 
failure of station equipment, and/or the stations associated piping.  Specifically, failure to complete identified work would 
reduce the integrity of the system and potentially result in significant customer outages.

Proactive Regulator Stations-NL-05 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
NL-05 is a 1964 vintage station located in Massapequa, New York consisting of one primary vault and one secondary vault. 
The inlet pressure to the stations is 99 psig and the outlet pressure of the station is 60 psig.  NL-05 is a two-stage station with 
a 6” Mooney FlowMax regulator in the primary stage vault and a 6” Fisher 1098 EGR regulator in the second stage vault with 
no third layer of Over Pressure Protection (OPP) currently installed.  The current condition of this station calls for a station 
rebuild consisting of the following work scope: 
 
• 
Replacement of all station main from station inlet valve to station outlet valve 
• 
Installation of new primary and secondary vaults 
• 
Installation of new station inlet and outlet valves, primary and secondary regulators, a strainer, and OPP.  
• 
Installation of new control lines  
 
The project will include the design completion, material procurement, easement aacquisition, permitting, construction, and 
testing of the new regulator station.  The project will replace the existing station, which is out of capacity, and will build a 
new station that is designed to the current standards including dual runs and OPP.   
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years. Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data. This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for NL-05.   
 
Customer Benefits: 
The current max flow through the station is 622.973 MCFH.  Rebuilding the regulating station will improve the asset condition 
and the safety of the asset, and address any open reliability concerns. A third layer of overpressure protection will be added 
to protect the station in an emergency.

Proactive Regulator Stations-NL-08 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
NL-08 is a 1956 vintage station located in Muttontown, New York consisting of a safety, district, and meter vault. The inlet 
pressure to the stations is 99 psig and the outlet pressure of the station is 60 psig.  NL-08 is a two-stage station with a 6” 
Fisher 1098 EGR/30 regulator in the first stage and a 6” Fisher 1098 EGR regulator in the second stage with no third layer of 
Over Pressure Protection (OPP) currently installed.  The existing condition of the station calls for a full station rebuild 
consisting of: 
 
• Replacement of existing station piping from the station inlet valve to the station outlet valve 
• Installation of new station inlet and outlet valves, bypass valves, regulators, and OPP  
• Installation of a new strainer  
• Installation of Roxtec seals in all through wall penetrations  
• Removal of existing meter vault and installation of OPP vault  
• Installation of Bilco doors, new vent poles with protective bollards 
• Installation of new telemetry and instrumentation and controls equipment 
  
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years.  Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for NL-08.    
 
Current condition of the station: 
• 
Water was present in both primary and secondary vaults.  
• 
Mains and control lines are embedded in concrete.  
• 
Atmospheric corrosion was observed on main, control lines and regulators components.  
• 
No adequate pipe supports are installed. 
• 
Station lacks proper venting. Vents pipes are flush to grade.  
• 
Chimney is constructed of bricks.  
• 
Transmitters and control cabinets are located aboveground. 
• 
No third layer of protection is installed.  
• 
Main and control lines are protected.

Proactive Regulator Stations - NL-20 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
NL-20 (Elmont Gate Station) is a 1965 vintage station located on National Grid property in Elmont, New York.  The station 
consists of safety, district, and orifice pit vaults.  The inlet pressure to the stations is 350 psig and the outlet pressure of the 
station is 60 psig.  NL-20 is a two-stage station with a 4” Fisher 1098H EGR/30 regulator in the first stage and a 6” Fisher 1098H 
EGR/30 regulator in the second stage and no third layer of Over Pressure Protection (OPP) currently installed.  This project 
calls for the refurbishment of the station and the addition of OPP.   
 
Work Scope will include: 
• Installation of new dual run station piping from the station inlet valve to the station outlet valve 
• Installation of new primary, secondary and OPP vaults  
• Installation of new station inlet and outlet valves, regulators, and addition of OPP  
• Installation of a new strainer  
• Installation of Roxtec seals in all through wall penetrations  
• Installation of Bilco doors and new vent poles 
• Installation of new telemetry and instrumentation control equipment 
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years. Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for NL-20.    
 
Current condition of the station: 
• 
Existing remote operated inlet valve is antique and needs to be replaced.  
• 
Existing scrubber is buried between inlet and primary vaults.  
• 
Atmospheric corrosion was observed on main, control lines and regulators components.  
• 
Existing pipe supports are inadequate. 
• 
No OPP is currently installed.  
• 
Temperature restriction for this station is 20 degrees. 
 
 
Customer Benefits: 
Type:   ☒  Project  ☐ Program

Proactive Regulator Stations-QL-24 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
QL-24 is a 1992 vintage station located at West Hempstead, New York consisting of one primary and one secondary vault. 
The inlet pressure to the station is 99 psig and the outlet pressure of the station is 60 psig.  QL-24 consists of a 3” Fisher 1098 
EGR regulator in the first stage and a 3” Fisher 1098 EGR regulator in the second stage with no third layer of protection.  The 
current condition of this regulating station calls for a rebuild consisting of the following work scope.   
 
Work Scope: 
• 
Installation of new a primary vault 
• 
Installation of new station piping and control lines 
• 
Installation of a new station inlet valve, strainer, primary regulator, and over pressurization protection (OPP) 
• 
Installation of new telemetry and mechanical cabinets 
• 
Installation of new vent poles  
• 
Installation of new access manholes and ladder systems 
• 
Installation of pipe supports  
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years.  Using data 
from the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; 
technical assessments were made for each pressure regulating station taking into account: pipe and equipment condition, 
operating pressure, regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer 
impact resulting from a station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for QL-24.    
 
Current condition of the station: 
• 
Atmospheric corrosion observed on piping, control lines and regulator components  
• 
No adequate pipe supports currently installed  
• 
Dresser couplings observed within both the primary and secondary vaults  
• 
No third layer of protection is installed  
• 
Temperature restriction for this station is 20 degrees

Proactive Regulator Stations - SL-09 - Retirement 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
SL-09, located in Bellport, New York and is a 1958 vintage station consisting of single regulator and a relief valve.  The inlet 
pressure to the station is 60 psig and the outlet pressure of the station is 10 psig.  Max flow through the station is 694.14 
MCFH.  The station consists of a 2” Fisher 99-SP regulator in the secondary vault and a 3” Fisher 1098-63EG relief valve in a 
vault.  This station is not cathodically protected.  The current station will be retired, and the corresponding 10 psig system 
will be upgraded to 60 psig.  
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years.  Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for SL-09. 
 
Customer Benefits:  
The retirement of SL-09 and upgrade of the corresponding 10 psig system to 60 psig will increase system reliability within the 
Bellport area and will be more cost efficient than other considered alternatives.

 Proactive Regulator Stations - SL-23 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
SL-23 is a 1987 vintage station located in Calverton, New York consisting of one primary and one secondary vault.  The inlet 
pressure to the station is 350 psi and the outlet pressure is 124 psi.  The station consists of a 6" Fisher 1098H EGR in the 
primary vault and a 6” Fisher 1098H EGR in the secondary vault with no third layer of Over Pressure Protection (OPP) currently 
installed.  The current condition of this station calls for a station rebuild consisting of the following work scope: 
 
Work Scope: 
• 
Replacement of all station main from station inlet valve to station outlet valve 
• 
Installation of new primary and secondary vaults 
• 
Installation of new station inlet and outlet valves, primary and secondary regulators, a strainer, and OPP.  
• 
Installation of new control lines 
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years.  Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for SL-23.  
  
Current condition of the station: 
• 
Single feed station.  
• 
Atmospheric corrosion was observed on all piping and regulator components. 
• 
Regulator components and pilots in the district vault were observed to be covered in ice.  
• 
No adequate pipe supports are installed.  
• 
Drip pot is present downstream of the station inlet valve. 
• 
No third layer of protection is installed. 
• 
Isolation valves were observed to the east of the station, adjacent to the old compressor building. 
• 
Temperature restriction for this station is 60 degrees.

Proactive Regulator Stations - SL-27 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
SL-27 is in Yaphank, New York and is a 1991 vintage station consisting of one primary and one secondary regulator vault.  The 
inlet pressure to the station is 350 psi and the outlet pressure of the station is 60 psi.  The station consists of a 3" Fisher 1098H 
EGR in the primary vault and a 3" Fisher 1098H-EGR/30 in the secondary vault.  There is currently no over pressurization 
protection (OPP) installed within the station.  
 
Work Scope: 
• 
Installation of a new OPP vault, primary vault, and secondary vault 
• 
Installation of new station piping and control lines 
• 
Installation of a new station inlet valve, station outlet valve, strainer, primary regulator, secondary regulator, and OPP 
• 
Installation of new vent poles  
• 
Installation of new access manholes and ladder systems 
• 
Installation of pipe supports within each vault 
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years. Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for SL-27.  
  
Current condition of the station: 
• 
Atmospheric corrosion present on all piping and regulator components. 
• 
Inadequate pipe supports within each vault 
• 
Dresser couplings currently installed downstream of the secondary vault. 
• 
Drip pot is installed downstream of the station inlet valve.  
• 
No third layer of protection is installed. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
The current max flow through the station is 306.17 MCFH. Rebuilding the regulating station will improve the asset condition 
and safety and address any open reliability concerns. A third layer of overpressure protection will be added to protect the 
station in an emergency.

ladders within each vault with ladder ups, replacement of vault lids and manhole covers, replacement of vent poles, installation 
of new pipe supports, replacement of control lines, installation of protective bollards to protect the telemetry cabinets.  This 
project alternative was not identified as the primary option because it does not appropriately address the reliability concerns 
currently present with this regulating station.  
 
Alternative 2: Do Nothing.  The consequences of not completing the work could ultimately result in loss of service to customers 
in Yaphank and neighbouring areas.

Proactive Regulator Stations - SL-31 
 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
SL-31 is a 1977 vintage station located at Riverhead, New York.  The regulating station consists of one (1) 4" Mooney Flowgrid 
DP primary regulator and one (1) 4" Fisher 1098H EGR secondary regulator.  The inlet and outlet pressures of the station are 
124 psig and 60 psig, respectively.  The station will be relocated to a nearby National Grid property due to multiple traffic 
incidents.  
 
Work Scope will include:   
• 
Removal of existing regulating station equipment  
• 
Installation of new 6” distribution main where existing station is located after station removal  
• 
Rebuild of SL-31 in the Riverhead Gas Yard  
 
Justification Summary:  
To determine the work plan, all regulating stations are inspected and risk ranked once every three (3) years.  Using data from 
the annual Performance Testing (PT), Cathodic Protection (CP) testing, risk assessments, and on-site inspections; technical 
assessments were made for each pressure regulating station considering: pipe and equipment condition, operating pressure, 
regulator performance, and corrosion data.  This information combined with the potential customer impact resulting from a 
station outage was used to prioritize and schedule project improvement for SL-31.  
  
Current condition of the station: 
The SL-31 regulating station is currently located on the side of a busy road and has been involved in several traffic incidents. 
The current location is dangerous for National Grid employees and contractors. The station will be relocated to improve 
worker safety as well as to better protect the asset.  
 
 
Customer Benefits: 
The relocation and rebuild of this regulating station will better protect the asset and improve station reliability.  The regulating 
station has a maximum flow of 170.301 MCFH.

 Purchase Meters (New/Replacements) 
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230000042 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Khan, Saadat 
 
Business Contact: Muhammad Tambra 
 
Work Description:  
This program includes the purchase, testing, processing, and delivery of gas meters and associated instrumentation, including 
Mandated Meter Test/Replacement Program, growth targets, and continued Customer Meter Services (CMS) operations.  The 
estimated number of meters required to support both programs for FY25 – FY28 are: 
 
FY25 
FY26 
FY27 
FY28 
16,608 
14,565 
15,386 
15,390 
 
National Grid is required to statistically sample and remove meters to be tested for accuracy each year.  The number of meters 
removed and tested is sufficient to assure a statistical confidence level of 95 percent.  Test results are entered into a program 
that performs the statistical calculations based on an approved American National Standards Institute (ANSI) Standard.  The 
NY Public Service Commission has set accuracy limits for residential and commercial meter types.  Meter groups that fall 
beyond the specified limits are placed in a retirement program and are subsequently removed from service and retired.  
 
This program does not include the installation of meters (see Meter Change program). 
 
Justification Summary:  
The primary driver for meter and metering instrumentation purchases is compliance with state regulations governing meter 
accuracy and measurement of gas usage for customer bills.  PSC requirements stipulate a random sample and associated 
remediation/retirement program for installed gas meters.  In addition to the mandated meter change program, meters are 
required to support customer connection targets, as well as to support CMS operational requirements (load change, meter 
and/or service relocations, damage, & stopped meters) 
 
Meter manufacturers are phasing away from the traditional diaphragm meters to smart meters.  Itron has stopped producing 
the residential 250-class diaphragm meters since December 2021 and moved to smart meters exclusively.  Other 
manufacturers will follow suit, and National Grid will eventually be forced to use the vendor smart meters. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
• 
Metering and billing accuracy 
• 
Fewer unplanned service interruptions 
• 
Ensure meters meet regulatory standards

RCV - LTLI20739 - Dix Hills - RCV 047163210 
Work Description: 
Install remote controlled valve (RCV) on the 24" valve (#047163210) on the Long Island Expressway (LIE) south service road 
to isolate the southern 20" line, which runs from Dix Hills to the Deer Park RCVs.  Deer Park RCVs will be installed in FY23. 
Justification Summary: 
The RCV program was developed to rapidly isolate sections of main following pipeline failures. The gas industry has 
experienced incidents around the country where the response time in isolating the affected main segment was deemed too 
long and contributed to increasing the scope of the incident and/or the release of gas. It is anticipated that federal and/or 
state regulatory agencies will require utilities and pipeline companies to reduce the response time when system events 
require main isolation. Currently, the only way to shut down pipelines is to dispatch a crew and manually close existing 
pipeline valves, which could be time consuming. RCV program will install multiple RCVs throughout the natural gas 
transmission system to quickly respond to pipeline failures by safely and timely isolating portions of mains from the rest of 
the system. 
Customer Benefits: 
This project will help to maximize our ability to always maintain service to customers and allow for faster emergency response 
through remote operations of the valve.   The construction of the RCV in this location allows the National Grid to improve 
emergency response capability and reduce risk if a failure or line break occurs on a transmission main.  This selected option 
minimizes service interruptions for as many customers as possible while addressing a transmission system event.

RCV - LTLI20889 - Westbury2 
 
 
 
Work Description:  
This project will install a new 20" valve east of the valve cluster on the northern 20" line on the east side of Merrick Avenue, 
with the southern line to be completed in FY24 to facilitate reduced system isolation time in the event of a potential 
incident. 
 
Justification Summary:  
 The RCV program was developed to rapidly isolate sections of main following pipeline failures. The gas industry has 
experienced incidents around the country where the response time in isolating the affected main segment was deemed too 
long and contributed to increasing the scope of the incident and/or the release of gas. It is anticipated that federal and/or 
state regulatory agencies will require utilities and pipeline companies to reduce the response time when system events 
require main isolation. Currently, the only way to shut down pipelines is to dispatch a crew and manually close existing 
pipeline valves, which could be time consuming.  The RCV program will install multiple RCVs’ throughout the natural gas 
ttransmission system to quickly respond to pipeline failures by safely and timely isolating portions of mains from the rest of 
the system. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
This project will help to maximize our ability to always maintain service to customers and allow for faster emergency 
response through remote operations of the valve.   The construction of the RCV in this location allows the National Grid to 
improve emergency response capability and reduce risk if a failure or line break occurs on a transmission main.  This 
selected option minimizes service interruptions for as many customers as possible while addressing a transmission system 
event.

Replace Pipe on Bridges  
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230101510 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Khan, Saadat 
Business Contact: Fong, Carmen 
 
Work Description: 
This program will replace and/or remediate gas pipe on bridges (or any structures elevated more than four feet above 
grade) due to specific integrity concerns identified through corrosion inspections. 
 
This program will initially target the projects listed below: 
• 
Atlantic Ave, East Rockaway; and 
• 
Pulaski Rd, Northport. 
 
Justification Summary:  
The gas distribution system includes aboveground piping that crosses roads, railroads, waterways, other municipal facilities, 
and other geographic features where underground installations were not installed or possible.  These crossings may be 
supported by Company or third-party bridges or other structures at the beginning and end of the crossing. 
 
As these facilities are exposed to atmospheric conditions, Corrosion Control is responsible for the physical inspection and 
maintenance of each crossing every three (3) years for external corrosion prevention.   Corrosion Control also performs 
routine maintenance and repairs of the pipe coating and support system.  When the physical condition of the crossing pipe 
or support system is beyond normal maintenance, or when the cost and complexity exceed Corrosion Control’s responsibility, 
capital expenditures are required to perform major repairs or replacements to extend the facility life, to design and construct 
alternate facilities to maintain the integrity of the existing crossing or to install alternate crossings to maintain customer 
service. 
 
Expenditures required for these pipes on bridges and other structures are not covered by other pipe programs such as, but 
not limited to, Proactive Main Replacement – Leak Prone Pipe and Large Diameter Pipe Rehabilitation. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
This project will help improve the reliability and enhance safety of gas infrastructure.

RNG Interconnections - #1 
 
 
 
Type:    ☒ Project    ☐  Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230101807 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect  ☒ FOH 
Annual Program: ☐ 
Estimated Start Date: In-Progress 
Estimated In-Service Date: 3/31/2027 
Sponsor: Kileti, Pradheep 
Business Contact: Barkwill, Brian  
 
Work Description:    
This project will support the interconnection of a renewable natural gas project in KEDLI. The project is expected to produce 
approximately 450 dth/day into our adjacent 60# system and is considered shovel ready. This would offset gas consumption 
and reduces carbon emissions associated with customer use in that area.  
 
Justification Summary:  
The second largest wastewater treatment plant on Long Island is looking to inject RNG into the adjacent distribution network. 
The Project will reduce the treatment plant’s carbon footprint /associated emissions and minimize impacts to the local 
community. There is already a joint venture consulting team in place to evaluate design option, but the project is expected 
to produce approximately 450 dth/day. This would offset gas consumption and reduces carbon emissions associated with 
customer use in that area.  
 
The interconnection itself is projected to cost $5.305 million and the cost of piping is projected to cost $0.166 million for 
approximately 1,000 feet of pipe, for a total estimated cost of $5.471 million.  
Exhibit____(GIOP-5) 
Page 221 of 273


--- Page 351 ---
The RNG Interconnection system ensures any renewable natural gas has been properly conditioned to meet pipeline 
quality. The following are the critical components and systems of the RNG Interconnect: 
 
Gas Quality 
The Companies require gas chromatographs and sulfur/moisture analyzers to monitor sales gas from an RNG conditioning 
system to ensure it always meets gas quality requirements. Any instance where out of specification gas is measured, an 
actuated flow control valve would be shut preventing injection.  
 
Pressure Regulation  
The Companies require pressure to be regulated from a higher pressure, down to the operating pressure of the gas network 
Equally important, a third layer of protection to avoid over pressurization of the gas network is included in the RNG 
Interconnect. This third layer of protection will help prevent high-profile gas accidents caused by human error with sensing 
lines. 
 
Odorization 
RNG is a colorless and odorless (post conditioning) gas supply. For health and safety purposes, utilites must keep odorant 
levels at detectable levels to alert the public of gas leaks. To safely inject RNG, it must be odorized.  
  
Sales Meter / Control Room 
To provide gas safely and reliably to all of our customers at all times, the Companies’ Gas Operations teams need real time 
data about production from any RNG facility. In the event of a failure of the biogas production/conditioning system or if gas 
that is delivered to the Companies does not comport with applicable quality specifications, RNG injection will stop and gas 
Operations will update the need in real time to ensure uninterrupted service via other natural gas supplies.  
 
Piping & Valving 
RNG interconnections connect a conditioning system to the Companies’ natural gas distribution system. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
These projects are a vital part of the KEDNY-KEDLI Clean Energy Vision and the CLCPA. Locally sourcing renewable natural 
gas in lieu of geological natural gas is crucial towards reducing emissions and achieving the ambitious climate goals set by 
both the State and National Grid.  The WWTP is expected to produce over 450 dth/day, reducing carbon emissions by 
38,290 MT of CO2 eq/year.  
 
Funding Detail

Alternative 1:  The company could opt to do nothing, accepting the business and reputational hit associated with continued 
100% fossil gas supply. This project is taking unavoidable waste and beneficially using that waste to offset fossil gas. That’s not 
a recommended approach and would go against all the goals established in the CLCPA.  
 
Alternative 2: The Company could work with customers to electrify the heating system. Customers would be tasked with 
covering the costs for that transition. 
 
NPA Suitability Screening 
 
1. Is the project construction expected to commence and be completed within 24 months? 
☐ Yes                 ☐ No

☐ Yes                 ☐ No

☐ Yes                 ☐ No

NPA Evaluation 
 
Has this project been reviewed against the Company’s NPA Screening and Suitability Criteria? 
☐ Yes                 ☒ No 
 
Please briefly explain the results: The injection of renewable natural gas reduces the need for remote interstate production 
of natural gas, gas transmission and delivery to the East Rockaway, NY community on Long Island.  
 
CLCPA/GHG Analysis 
1. Is the project/program located in an area designated as a disadvantaged community (DAC)1? ☐ Yes    ☒ No 
2. If so, explain how was the routing determined and how will the project impact the surrounding area?  The 
wastewater treatment plant is an existing facility. For practical purposes, the gas can only be utilized by the adjacent 
area. With the injection of RNG, the area will utilize less geological natural gas.   
3. Will the project contribute to GHG emission reductions on the Company’s gas network? ☒ Yes    ☐ No 
4. Please explain the project’s GHG impact.    
 
RNG Interconnection 1 is expected to offset 165,000 MMBtu/year of natural gas use. This primary pathway for reducing 
emission stems from no longer needed to procure natural gas for that demand. Upstream emissions includes losses 
associated with the production, gathering, boosting, storage and transmission of the natural gas chain. According to the 
paper, Life Cycle Analysis of Natural Gas Extraction and Power Generation, the national average emissions associated with 
natural gas from collection through consumption is 30.9 g CO2e/MJ (20-year GWP). In the Joint Utilities Proposal for an 
Annual Greenhouse Gas Emissions Inventory Report, only 10% of the emissions are associated with distribution companies 
and consumer use, making upstream emissions 27.81 g CO2e/MJ.  
1 As defined in Environmental Conservation Law Section 75-0101(5), disadvantaged communities are “communities 
that bear burdens of negative public health effects, environmental pollution, impacts of climate change, and possess 
certain socioeconomic criteria, or comprise high-concentrations of low- and moderate- income households, as

For each MMBtu sourced locally, the project reduced emissions associated with upstream supply. Below is the math 
outlining the emissions benefits: 
165,000 (MMBtu/Year) x 1055.0559 (MJ/ MMBtu) x 27.81 (g CO2/MJ) x (1MT/1,000,000 g) = 4,841 MT CO2 avoided. 
Additionally, this project qualified for the renewable fuel standard program. The environmental credits associated with this 
project are significant. Using the (20-year GWP) GREET model, a –0.1448 MTCO2e / MMBtu factor can be used for 
wastewater sludge only RNG projects. Coupled with offset natural gas use (-.08832 MT CO2e/MMBtu). The expected 
emissions reductions are 38,290MT of CO2 eq/year. 
In total, this project has the ability to offset over 43,100 MT of CO2 if the environmental credits can be purchased.

RNG – Interconnections - #2 
 
 
 
Type:  ☒ Project    ☐  Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230101806 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect ☒ FOH 
Annual Program: ☐ 
Estimated Start Date: In-Progress 
Estimated In-Service Date: 3/31/2027 
Sponsor: Kileti, Pradheep 
Business Contact: Barkwill, Brian  
 
Work Description:    
This project will support the interconnection of a renewable natural gas project in KEDLI. The project is expected to produce 
approximately 400 dth/day into our adjacent 124# system and is considered shovel ready. This would offset gas consumption 
and reduces carbon emissions associated with customer use in that area.  
 
Justification Summary: 
A 2,900 acre development in Suffolk County has been rezoned in Calverton, NY. The planned industrial development project 
will include a food waste to RNG facility, initially slated to produced up to 400 dth/day with plans to double production to 
800 dth/day. This would offset gas consumption and reduces carbon emissions associated with customer use in that area. 
 
The interconnection itself is projected to cost $5.305 million and the cost of piping is projected to cost $0.432 million for 
approximately 2,600 feet of pipe, for a total estimated cost of $5.737 million.  
 
 
Exhibit____(GIOP-5) 
Page 225 of 273


--- Page 355 ---
The RNG Interconnection system ensures any renewable natural gas has been properly conditioned to meet pipeline 
quality. The following are the critical components and systems of the RNG Interconnect: 
 
Gas Quality 
The Companies require gas chromatographs and sulfur/moisture analyzers to monitor sales gas from an RNG conditioning 
system to ensure it always meets gas quality requirements. Any instance where out of specification gas is measured, an 
actuated flow control valve would be shut preventing injection.  
 
Pressure Regulation  
The Companies require pressure to be regulated from a higher pressure, down to the operating pressure of the gas network 
Equally important, a third layer of protection to avoid over pressurization of the gas network is included in the RNG 
Interconnect. This third layer of protection will help prevent high-profile gas accidents caused by human error with sensing 
lines. 
 
Odorization 
RNG is a colorless and odorless (post conditioning) gas supply. For health and safety purposes, utilites must keep odorant 
levels at detectable levels to alert the public of gas leaks. To safely inject RNG, it must be odorized.  
  
Sales Meter / Control Room 
To provide gas safely and reliably to all of our customers at all times, the Companies’ Gas Operations teams need real time 
data about production from any RNG facility. In the event of a failure of the biogas production/conditioning system or if gas 
that is delivered to the Companies does not comport with applicable quality specifications, RNG injection will stop and gas 
Operations will update the need in real time to ensure uninterrupted service via other natural gas supplies.  
 
Piping & Valving 
RNG interconnections connect a conditioning system to the Companies’ natural gas distribution system. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
These projects are a vital part of the KEDNY-KEDLI Clean Energy Vision and the CLCPA. Locally sourcing renewable natural 
gas in lieu of geological natural gas is crucial towards reducing emissions and achieving the ambitious climate goals set by 
both the State and National Grid.  This facility will produce up to 400 dth/day reducing carbon emissions by 74,521 MT of 
CO2 eq/year.

4. Please explain the project’s GHG impact.    
 
RNG Interconnection 2 is expected to offset 146,000 MMBtu/year of natural gas use. This primary pathway for reducing 
emission stems from no longer needed to procure natural gas for that demand. Upstream emissions includes losses 
associated with the production, gathering, boosting, storage and transmission of the natural gas chain. According to the 
paper, Life Cycle Analysis of Natural Gas Extraction and Power Generation, the national average emissions associated with 
natural gas from collection through consumption is 30.9 g CO2e/MJ (20-year GWP). In the Joint Utilities Proposal for an 
Annual Greenhouse Gas Emissions Inventory Report, only 10% of the emissions are associated with distribution companies 
and consumer use, making upstream emissions 27.81 g CO2e/MJ.

Service Replacement - Reactive – Leaks 
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230000025 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date:  
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Khan, Saadat 
Business Contact: Qiu, DongNi 
 
Work Description:  
The reactive service replacement program consists of non-discretionary work randomly generated through public leak 
reports, programmed leak surveys, mandated activities, and customer-generated requests.     
 
Justification Summary:  
The goal of this program is to reduce the risk associated with leaks on existing services to enhance the safety and reliability 
of the Company’s system.  The program provides funding for the reactive replacement of gas services to address work 
activities outside the normal scope of the integrity, reliability, public works, and growth programs.  The proactive main and 
service replacement programs upgrade existing customer services prioritized in the main replacement program by risk based 
on pressure, material, vintage, location, and select other variables.  The reactive service replacement program addresses 
leakage and other maintenance activities on the remaining services. 
 
The program budget consists of costs to replace leaking services, damages, service abandonments due to inactivity or 
demolition requests, customer-driven relocations of existing services, and other substandard conditions. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
This program will reduce the risk associated with these services and reduce leaks and emissions.

Take Station - Over Pressure Protection - Long Beach  
 
 
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230101591 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐ 
Estimated Start Date: 
01/01/2024 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
11/30/2024 
Sponsor: Varga, Brian  
 
Business Contact: Adesina, Risikat  
 
Work Description: 
 The Long Beach Take Station is in Long Beach, New York. The station has an inlet pressure of 1000 psig and consists of four 
(4) separate regulating stations (QL-15, QL-16, QL-17, and QL-11).  QL-15, QL-16, QL-17 are all 1000 psig to 450 psig stations 
and QL-11 is a 450 psig to 60 psig station.  There is currently no over pressurization protection (OPP) installed within the Long 
Beach Take Station.  
 
Justification Summary:  
National Grid systems are susceptible to any accidental over-pressurization from pipeline suppliers. National Grid has 
committed to having OPP installed within all take stations by 2027.  
 
Customer Benefits: 
The addition of OPP to the Long Beach Take Station will allow National Grid to ensure safe and reliable service to all customers, 
and prevent against the risk of future over-pressurization on the system.

Take Station - Over Pressure Protection - South Commack 
 
 
 
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230101592 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect

Business Contact: Adesina, Risikat  
 
Work Description:  
The South Commack Take Station is in Commack, New York. The station has an inlet pressure of 1440psi and consists of four 
(4) separate regulating stations (SL-36, SL-37, SL-69, and SL-70). SL-36, and SL-37 are 1440 psig to 350 psig stations and SL-
69, and SL-70 are 1440 psig to 450 psig stations. There is currently no over pressurization protection (OPP) installed within 
the South Commack Take Station. 
 
Justification Summary:  
National Grid systems are susceptible to any accidental over-pressurization from pipeline suppliers. National Grid has 
committed to having OPP installed within all take stations by 2027.  
  
Customer Benefits: 
 The addition of OPP to the South Commack Take Station will allow National Grid to ensure safe and reliable service to all 
customers, and prevent against the risk of future over-pressurization on the system.

Telecom – Reactive 
 
 
 
Type:    ☐Project    ☒  Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 
5230000115 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI 
 
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☒ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date: 
Sponsor: Backus, Katie 
Business Contact: McCarthy, Kenneth 
 
Work Description:  
This program funds capital repairs and replacements of telecom equipment and installations. 
 
Justification Summary:  
The DNY Telecom department operates and maintains radio and fiber equipment that support dispatch operations, power 
plants and liquified natural gas (LNG) facilities, SCADA communication / field telemetry, and automated meter reading 
(AMR).  
 
Telecom reactive projects are unplanned capital work resulting from emergency situations, including ,but not limited to, 
third party damage and external events (weather, vehicle, power surges, etc.). The Telecom reactive program supports 
capital replacements that must be completed at or near the time of discovery to maintain safe and reliable communications 
across the gas business unit.  
 
The Telecom Reactive funding request is primarily based on historic experience and funding levels (previously under the 
Telecom Damaged Failure line item), with inflation. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
Continued field crew efficiencies as the result of direct and reliable communication across National Grid’s telecom network 
to facilitate Timely and reliable response to weather events, such as flood-driven automatic meter shutoffs.

Tools & Equipment  
 
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230000117, 5230101118 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☒ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒  
Estimated Start Date:  
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Malone, David  
Business Contact: Auriemma, Mattia  
 
Work Description:  
KEDLI’s Purchase of Miscellaneous Capital Tools and Equipment budget is for purchases that are not used for specific projects. 
These items support the safety of our employees, our customers, and the general public. The items provide cost efficiencies 
across multiple mandated programs, commitments to customer needs and expectations, and will allow the potential increase 
of productivity for on-going day-to-day operations of the gas business unit.  
 
This budget item is typically used to support process related initiatives and assure subsequent goals are achieved within the 
entire Gas organization. These items relate to safety (i.e. mechanized maintenance of traffic devices, worker safety 
enhancements etc.), climate change (i.e. apparatus to minimize emissions through natural gas drawdown operations), 
support of new, emerging and on-going technologies (i.e. capital spares and parts for trenchless and keyhole technologies), 
and the initiation of innovative applications that will lead to improved operations. 
 
Justification Summary:  
Current Company policy capitalizes general tool and/or equipment purchases subject to predetermined minimal dollar 
thresholds ($500 for KEDLI). Such general equipment includes tooling (hand, power, pneumatic, hydraulic, etc.), specialty 
equipment, PPE, office machines, electronic data processing equipment and software applications, shop and garage 
equipment and communications. The Purchase Miscellaneous Capital Tools and Equipment line item captures the above 
mentioned items that are not used for specific projects but rather support the safe, efficient, and on-going day-to-day 
operations of the gas business unit.  Purchase of Miscellaneous Capital Tools and Equipment utilize project numbers that are 
budgeted based on historical funding due to the inability to associate this equipment with any one specific project. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
• 
Improved public safety due to mechanized maintenance of traffic devices and public safety enhancements.  
• 
Noise reduction enhancements with new technology tooling. 
• 
Productivity increases and potential unit cost reductions. 
• 
Compliance with federal and state code requirements including new US Department of Transportation (USDOT)

Tools & Equipment - Meter Testing Equipment  
 
 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230000118 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☒ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☒ 
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
Sponsor: Khan, Saadat 
 
Business Contact: Muhammad Tambra 
 
Work Description:  
This program includes purchasing equipment to support the testing of gas meters and replacing meter lab equipment to 
ensure safety, compliance, and meter testing accuracy.  State regulations require random sampling of gas meter performance 
on an annual basis.  Meters are classified based on manufacture/model, and the population size determines the number of 
meters tested.  Accuracy and leak testing of KEDLI’s gas meters are performed at the Company’s meter lab.  This program 
provides funding for the purchase of meter testing equipment to support the testing of gas meters for accuracy and potential 
leaks, in addition to replacing meter testing equipment that has reached its end of life. 
 
Justification Summary:  
The primary driver for meter testing equipment purchases is compliance with state regulations governing gas meter testing 
accuracy and the continued support of meter testing programs. 
 
Customer Benefits: 
• 
Metering and billing accuracy 
• 
Ensure meters meet safety standards

Transmission Service Integrity 
Type: ☐ Project ☒ Program
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M
Investment Code: Transmission Customer Services 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☐ Mandated ☒ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect
Annual Program: ☒
Estimated Start Date: 
Estimated In-Service Date: 
Sponsor: Leyble, Dennis 
Business Contact: Risikat Adesina 
Work Description: 
There are high volume customers that receive gas directly from National Grid transmission mains.  This program will ensure 
the integrity of the transmission customer services by: 
•
Identifying all transmission services
•
Performing risk assessment of the pressure regulating assets
•
Risk ranking the assets
•
Developing work plan to overhaul services as needed
•
Executing a work plan
This program will also add a third layer of protection to each of these assets. 
Justification Summary: 
Ensuring integrity on all gas transmission assets is of paramount importance to National Grid.  Individual transmission 
customer services are fed from Company-owned and operated piping, regulating stations and metering before the gas enters 
customer-owned piping.  These National Grid facilities must be operated, maintained, and overhauled to the Company 
standards.  Currently, these facilities are not risk assessed or scheduled for overhaul on an established schedule like the 
proactive regulator stations.   
The process safety management system at National Grid is a risk management tool used to analyze and prevent the release 
of natural gas.   An incident could lead to the loss of control in operation of an asset, resulting in significant loss of containment 
of a dangerous substance presenting potential safety and environmental issues. 
Customer Benefits: 
There are numerous high-volume customers across the Long Island gas system that require a direct transmission service due 
to their gas needs.  Under this program, necessary remediation work will be performed on such service stations to ensure 
their continued safe and reliable operation.  
Funding Detail 
Cost Breakdown: 
No spend is allocated within this program in FY25 and FY26 because an internal risk rating register of potential projects is 
ongoing.

Transmission Station Integrity - PHMSA - Stewart 
Ave - NL-15 & NL-16 Phase 1  
 
Type: ☒ Project ☐ Program 
Category: ☒ Capital  ☐ Both Capital & O&M 
 
Investment Code: 5230101307 
Region: ☐ KEDNY ☒ KEDLI  
Planning Portfolio: ☐ Customer Connections ☒ Mandated ☐ Reliability ☐ Non-Infrastructure ☐ Indirect 
Annual Program: ☐ 
Estimated Start Date: 
01/01/2023 
Estimated In-Service Date:  
11/30/2024 
Sponsor: 
Leyble, Dennis  
Business Contact: 
Adesina, Risikat  
 
Work Description:  
NL-15 & NL-16 are located at Hempstead, New York and are 1959 vintage stations consisting of aboveground safety and 
district regulator. The inlet pressure to the stations is 350 psig and the outlet pressure of the stations is 99 psig.  NL-15/NL-16 
are a two-stage station consisting of two (2) 6” Fisher 1098H EGR/30 in series and no third layer of Over Pressure Protection 
(OPP).  Loss of the two stations could potentially impact thousands of customers.  NL-16 is located adjacent to NL-15 station 
both projects will be developed at the same time.  
 
Work Scope for both stations will include:  
•
